would delete free lab unlocks (T1 W30 = `Labs` free vs `10 Gems` premium), which cannot happen. So a fully reached tier
with a pass owned has **46** claim cells (23 waves Ã— 2 tracks) â†’ [H038](../hypotheses/H038-milestone-two-track-badge.md)
turns it into a badge-count test.

**The re-added ledger (T1â€“T12).** Free track: **4 675 gems** (Î£ per tier 235 Â· 180 Â· 205 Â· 320 Â· 275 Â· 390 Â· 340 Â· 490 Â·
450 Â· 535 Â· 610 Â· 645) and **540 stones**; premium track on top: **7 850 gems** and **1 380 stones**. Both tracks =
**12 525 gems + 1 920 stones = 31 725 gem-equivalents**, of which the four Premium Passes are **21 650 ge** (pass values

(8 570 â†’ **7 850**, 1 480 â†’ **1 380**).

10 / 70 stones, 50 k / 11.5 m coins. Sample reconstructions: T1 free coins = 25 + 150 + 1 500 = 1 675; T1 free gems =
10+15+20+20+20+25+25+30+30+30+30 = 235; T1 premium coins = 50 + 300 + 600 + 2 500 + 30 000 + 100 000 + 8 000 = 141 450;
T4 premium coins = 100 k+150 k+200 k+250 k+300 k+1 m+1.5 m+8 m = 11.5 m.

**Named free relic per tier** (W4 500): Coins +2 % Â· Lab Speed +1.5 % Â· Critical Factor +2 % Â· Damage +2 % Â· Health +1.5 % Â·
Defense Absolute +2 % Â· Coins +5 % Â· Damage/Meter +5 % Â· Health +5 % Â· Damage/Meter +5 % Â· Defense Absolute +10 % Â·
Lab Speed +10 % for T1â†’T12, with **stones** (15â†’40) in the premium cell of the same wave â€” the second half of T018's four
free relic pipelines.

**Coin columns, quantified:** free **35.7 m** coins through T12 vs premium **989 m** â€” the reason the coin cells stay

Bounty 13 110 / Scout 13 850 / Fetch 14 480** bits â‡’ â‰ˆ **80 k bits** for the whole set â€” a gem-free economy.

(Î£ `n(n+1)/2`: Bounty 99 lv = **4 950** âœ“), cooldown lines **2 bits/level** (Î£ `n(n+1)`: 60 lv = **3 660** âœ“ on both
Bounty and Fetch), targets **100 bits/level** (9 lv = **4 500** âœ“), and Fetch's two stat lines **4 700** / **6 120** âœ“;
the Bounty sum **13 110** equals Tower Hub's independently printed chip total, and Fetch's droptable sums to exactly

**medal 2 % (cap 10)**, reroll shard 3 %, the four type shards **1 % each**, common module 0.4 % (cap 5), rare 0.1 %
(cap 2), with **coins and shards uncapped**. At max (50 % find / 50 % double / 60 s) that is 1 440 procs â†’ 720 finds/day
â‡’ **20 gems + 10 medals + â‰ˆ 75 shard-units/day**, so the caps bind on gems/medals but not on shards â€” the argument for

target at death*, so it must fire inside the 200-s GT / 100-s BH / 200-s DW window (T008 Â§3) and Summon's cash bonus
stacks on Golden Tower (GT multiplies cash too) and feeds GT+.

**Preset/bit-refund economy:** `Presets` lab L5 = 2 presets, Vault node +3 = 5 **and removes the weekly respec limit**;
switching presets **costs or refunds the bit difference**; the Guardian respec is 150 gems â†’ **100 â†’ 51 â†’ 0** via the
Vault nodes (T017), refunding chip/slot bits but not the 200 unlock bits. Each owned Guardian skin = **+0.6 % coins**.

**Binary anchors:** `GuardianChips`, `GuardianPresetsUI`, `GuardianPresetData`, `RespecGuardianUI`, `SummonPortal(Manager)`,
`ScoutPingVfx`, and the UI string `Fetch Ability`.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 25 (iteration 34: the lab layer's two meta-labs)

## Asked

The missing pieces of the research-time economy: the two meta-labs' exact ladders, how their bonuses combine with relics,
and whether the rush-price hypothesis (H035) could be settled from sources.

## What was established (T006 Rev 3, Verified; H035 resolved)

**Both meta-labs are 99-level and share one coin curve** (printed identically on both pages): 40 / 83 / 211 / 522 / 1 116 /
2 099 / 3 579 / 5 667 / 8 474 / 12 116 â‡’ **Î£10 = 33 907 coins** and **6 h 32 m** of cumulative base time.
`Labs Speed` = **+0.02Ã—/level â‡’ Ã—2.98** (1 + 0.02Â·99 âœ“, T1 W150); `Labs Coin Discount` = **âˆ’0.30 %/level â‡’ âˆ’29.7 %**
(0.30Â·99 âœ“, T1 W30); maxing one costs **472 456 246 coins** and **237 689 238 s** of base time (2 751 days â‰ˆ 7.5 yr of a
single slot at 1Ã—).

**Stacking is multiplicative:** `effective = base Ã· (research bonus Ã— relic bonus)` â€” the source's own example
1.25 Ã— 2.98 = **3.725** (not 3.23) âœ“. So lab-speed relics multiply the research bonus rather than adding, which promotes them
in T013's event-shop ranking; and a `3 d 11 h` gate line becomes **22.3 h** at Ã—3.725.

**Two quirks:** `Labs Speed` does not accelerate **its own** remaining levels (relics do) and does not advance the â€œlab timeâ€
event mission; `Labs Coin Discount` does not discount itself. Practically: start the speed line first in a session, take
**10-level blocks** of each, speed first while online, discount first while banking coins.

**H035 resolved** by two independent sources: the printed **v0.17 rush table** (base 7.5 gems/h with bucket discounts
0.91 / 0.79 / 0.66 â‡’ 163 / 1 000 / 3 550 gems = 6.79 / 5.95 / 4.93 per hour) reproduces arithmetically
(7.5Â·24Â·0.91 = 163.8 etc.), and the completion bonus is **+0.015Ã— per gold-boxed lab** â€” so rush after gold-boxing a
category, and always in the largest single bucket.

## Also confirmed

5 slots for **4 900 gems** (restated from T009 Â§1) â€” the queue this technique packs; and the boost table's flat pricing
(Ã—1.5 = 30 cells/skipped hour) still beats every gem bucket except the â‰¥ 30-day ones.

## Verdict

T006 **Rev 3**, **Verified (arithmetic)** â€” the 6th numeric doc. No new hypotheses this pass (the two open lab questions
are now H035's optional confirmation reads plus the still-open H009 elite-density measurement). Next: the Prestige/
Experience tooltip read, or the per-category lab cost pages (`wiki/lab/utility-research` et al.) for line-by-line prices.

  25 / 50 / 75 / 100 %, a +2 line at 50 / 100 %.
- **The main-effect tail is the worst rate in the layer:** 16 038 âˆ’ 8 073 = **7 965** stones for points 71â€“100 =
  **265.5/point vs 115.3/point** for the first 70 % (**2.30Ã—** dearer) â‡’ buy it last, after checking the T19 W50 labs.
- **Per-slot budget = 28 711 stones** = 4 600 (unique ladder, includes the 1 000 unlock) + 16 038 + 8 073 â‰ˆ **11 UW sync
  packages**; the spend order is unlock â†’ main to 10 % (243) â†’ main to your FLOOR boundary (2 079 @ 34 %) â†’ unique tiers
  (1 000/1 200/1 400) â†’ sub-stat to the same boundary â†’ main tail.

**Pull economics (printed rates, derived EV):** Standard Banner 68.5 / 29 / 2.5 % (Î£ = 100.0 âœ“) â‡’ **1 Epic â‰ˆ 39.1 pulls
â‰ˆ 782 gems** with the 150-pull pity (0.975Â¹âµâ° â‰ˆ 0.0224), the â‰¥ 1-Rare-per-10-pull guarantee binds only **2.3 %** of the
time (`0.685Â¹â°`), and shards/pull = **8.575 early â†’ 31.325 late** â€” a **3.65Ã—** jump purely because a 5â˜… Ancestral makes

**The pull budget is a three-phase process, and its total reproduces the printed 2 560 exactly.** Inside a phase a named

at L4 and **847 / 16 940** for a named Rare at L4; a deterministic expectation model (mass renormalised whenever a class
empties) returns **241 / 848 / 1 200 / 1 808 / 2 561** against the closed forms 240 / 847 / 1 200 / 1 807 / **2 560** âœ“.
Consequences written into the doc: the naive â€œ96 copies Ã— 20 = 1 920 gemsâ€ for the six-card currency set is **8.8Ã— low**
(â‰ˆ 16 960 gems, 39 days at ~435 gems/day), and **the whole Common class reaching L4 costs the same 4 800 gems that one
Common at L4 does** â€” which is the quantitative justification for â€œlevels 1â€“4 wide, then widen the deckâ€.

**Prices verified against the printed tables:** the copy/gem columns (marginal 1/2/5/8/12/20/32 with gems 20/40/100/160/
240/400/640, totals 1/3/8/16/28/48/**80** and 20/60/160/320/560/960/**1 600**) reproduce row by row; the **slot ladder's
20 cumulative sums all add up** (50 â€¦ 8 500 â‡’ **48 400** to 21 slots, +10 000 = **58 400** at slot 22, 28 with Vault keys).

**New priced comparison across the whole currency set** (game-vault's printed L1â†’L7 values Ã· the marginal gems): gems per
point at L1â†’2 = **Cash 2.0 â†’ Enemy Balance 4 â†’ Coins 8 â†’ Critical Coin 13.3 â†’ Cells 20/cell â†’ Wave Skip 40 â†’ Free
Upgrades 40**, i.e. the three Common currency cards are cheaper twice over (more points per level *and* an 80 % mass vs
17 %). `Wave Skip`'s ladder is irregular (+1,+1,+2,+2,+2,+2) with its best step at **L3â†’4** (80 gems/pt).

**Source reconciliation:** game-vault's table is the **30-card** pool (12 C + 8 R + 10 E, 2 400 copies / 48 000 gems) and
the current pool adds exactly **Cells** (Rare) + **Area of Effect** (Epic) â‡’ 12+9+11 = 32 âœ“ and **2 400 + 2Â·80 = 2 560** âœ“ /
48 000 + 3 200 = 51 200 âœ“. So the two wikis are one version apart, not in conflict.

**The law, in one line:** Tower Hub states â€œevery level adds a fixed step, and **each level costs a fixed number of bits more
than the one before**â€ â‡’ `price(level n) = kÂ·n`, total `kÂ·n(n+1)/2`. With the printed `k` per line, **four of four printed chip
totals re-add exactly**: Attack **13 345** (25/1/100 at 19/90/9 levels), Bounty **13 110** (1/2/100 at 99/60/9), Ally
**14 470**, Scout **13 850**.

**Two caps sets are now *solved*, not printed.** Ally's Cooldown cap is pinned by its own ladder (120â†’30 s = 90 levels), and
the remaining two caps are the integer pair closing the printed total: `5Â·n(n+1)/2 + m(m+1)/2 = 10 375` â‡’ **(60, 49)**;
Scout's Cooldown = 70 levels (105â†’35 s) and `5Â·n(n+1)/2 + 10Â·m(m+1)/2 = 8 880` â‡’ **(16, 40)**, unique among the neighbours
(the remainders are non-pronic). Side effects: Ally's Max Recovery likely ends near **Ã—6.0** and Scout's Range near **Ã—3.6**,
both below the older summary values â†’ **H052**.

**A new table rule.** â€œShowing 10 of **N** rowsâ€ encodes the longest line's level count as **N âˆ’ 2** â€” verified on all four
chips whose caps are known (92â†’90, 101â†’99, 62â†’60, 72â†’70) â‡’ **Summon's longest line has 71 levels**, and since its Cooldown
starts at 40 s that must be the **Cash Bonus** line: **Ã—1.00 â†’ â‰ˆ Ã—72** at 100 bits/level â‡’ â‰ˆ **255 600 bits** in one line, the
largest bit sink in the layer.

**The buy order is now a `k` sort** (Â§2.2): `kÂ·55` bits buys the first 10 levels of a line, so **k = 1** (Bounty Multiplier,
+1 %/level) â†’ **k = 2** (Fetch/Summon/Scout Cooldown, Summon Duration) â†’ **k = 5** (Fetch Find, Ally Recovery, Scout Range) â†’
**k = 10** â†’ the **k = 100** Targets/Cash Bonus lines last. The whole `k â‰¤ 10` layer of the coin preset â‰ˆ **24 600 bits**
(Bounty 8 610 + Fetch 14 480 + Summon's two cheap lines â‰ˆ 1 500).

# 2026-11-02 - Session 36 (iteration 45: the bot layer budgeted in events)

## Asked

T015 Rev 2 had the three cost formulas but no **medal budget layer**: how much of a season's medal income the bot block
actually eats, and in which order the guide's own priority puts the lines.

## What was established (T015 Rev 3, Verified (arithmetic))

**The formulas reproduce a fifth total.** With the printed caps (Duration 30, Cooldown 15, Bonus 30, Range 20) the line
formula `20LÂ² + 80L` gives **20 400 + 5 700 + 20 400 + 9 600 = 56 100 âœ“** â€” Golden Bot's printed total. Adding the unlock
ladder **150 + 300 + 600 + 900 + 1 200 = 3 150 âœ“** and the per-level series **100/140/â€¦/460 â†’ Î£ 2 800 âœ“** closes the layer
without a single device read.

**Budget expressed in events (Ã· â‰ˆ 1 215 medals/event):** five unlocks **2.6 events** Â· theme + song **400/event** Â· Golden
Bot's four lines **46.2 events** Â· Golden Bot+ **14.4 events** (after **1 250 stones**) Â· all five Bot+ **49.5 events**.
So the coin-preset bot block is a **~1-year** project and the *first* 10 levels of each line (2 800 medals each) are where
the ratio is best.

**Priority, with reasons (from the guide):** unlock **one** bot and level it before unlocking the next (a fresh bot is nearly
worthless); **Golden** = Range then Bonus, keeping Range â‰ˆ 2 levels ahead, cooldown only to the sync divisor; **Amplify** =
Range first because range â€œraises the chance of the boss passing through the bot's areaâ€; **Thunder** must be **maxed** or
skipped; **Flame** last. Cosmetic line-items: **theme + song = 400 medals/event**, and the **3 songs each give +0.6 % coins**.
Range is doubly cheap because **all bot ranges are amplified by the tower's own range** (so the Range workshop line, card and
relic widen bot coverage for free).

**Version/UI notes recorded:** `Gilded Sniper` counts a kill as inside Golden Bot **only while the bot is active**; v28 made

# 2026-11-02 - Session 52 (iteration 61: both Vault trees close as closed forms and the cosmetic law lands at +90 %)

## Asked

Two open layers with printed tables but no derived forms: (a) do the Vault's Enemy and Power tab totals follow from their
per-level ladders rather than being transcribed, and (b) what is the *formula* behind the cosmetic coin line now that the
themes page prints it? (`wiki/system/the-vault`, verified 2026-10-28; `wiki/system/themes-menu`, 2026-11-01, v29.0.6.)

## What was established

**T017 Rev 4 - the Enemy tab is a single geometric ladder.** The page prints the ends of the 30-level node ladder
(**25** first, **103** last) and the node total (**1 662**). `round(25 Ã— 1.05^(nâˆ’1))` reproduces both ends **and** the sum:
`25(1.05^30 âˆ’ 1)/0.05 = 1 660.97` exact, **1 662** rounded level-by-level â‡’ one law, three independent prints. Hence
`36 nodes Ã— 1 662 = ` **59 832** for the whole Enemy tree âœ“ (and the page's â€œa full group of nine is 14 958 before the group
unlockâ€ = `9 Ã— 1 662` âœ“). Marginal read: 25 keys â†’ 103 keys for the same +1 %, so the 5-level knee rule of Â§2.2 applies.

**T017 Rev 4 - the Power tab closes instead of subtracting.** **39** of the 47 nodes are the standard
`10 / 20 / 40 / 70 / 110 = 250` ladder â‡’ `39 Ã— 250 = 9 750`; the cheapest node is `Ultimate Damage`
`5 / 10 / 20 / 35 / 55 = 125`; **seven** nodes start at 20 keys and carry the remaining
`11 395 âˆ’ 9 750 âˆ’ 125 = ` **1 520** (â‰ˆ 217 each â‡’ their ladders are not identical). This replaces Rev 3's â€œ240-key gap
unexplainedâ€ note: the gap is exactly the 20-key-start group, whose individual ladders are the one on-device read left.

**T013 Rev 5 - the cosmetic multiplier is a printed additive law.**
`1 + 0.004Â·(tower skins) + 0.008Â·(backgrounds) + 0.006Â·(menu + guardian + songs)`, displayed on the **tier multiplier
menu**. With the page's v29.0.6 counts: 83 Ã— 0.004 = **+33.2 pp**, 51 Ã— 0.008 = **+40.8 pp** (43 event + 8 guild
backgrounds), 8 Ã— 0.006 = +4.8 (menu), 15 Ã— 0.006 = +9.0 (guild guardians), 3 Ã— 0.006 = +1.8 (the shop songs; the game has
**44** songs but â€œonly the three shop songs countâ€) â‡’ **â‰ˆ +89.6 %**, i.e. the cosmetic line alone is a **Ã—1.90** coin
multiplier, comparable to the entire relic coin pool (+99 %, T018 Â§3) and free of any convexity.

**Pricing is calibrated, so cosmetics have no ordering trick.** `0.012 / 400 = 0.006 / 200 = 3.0e-5` multiplier per medal
â‡’ **0.30 pp per 100 medals** for a theme pair and for a song alike, **1 pp per 333 medals**, and the one-event bill
(400 + 200 Ã— 3 = **1 000 medals**) buys exactly **3.0 pp**. That is a new, arithmetic reason for the `medals-guide` order
that Rev 4 had taken on faith.

**Census note.** The transcribed subsets add to 24 milestone + 43 event + 8 guild = **75** tower skins against the printed
**83**; the 8-skin remainder is the three unconfirmed pairs (Neuron / Snowstorm / Valentine) plus gift/anniversary skins, so
the law is the trusted part and the counts are the panel read (falsifier: 44 counting songs would add +24.6 pp).

so **nine** of the eleven relics carry **48** of the 52 pp (a cheap line to finish). The general rule is printed next to it:
â€œbonuses from **similar** relics **add** together, while they **multiply** against other types of bonusesâ€ â€” the second print

line each until handled: `Increases â€¦` (plural, not `Increase â€¦`) and the unit rows `by 1m` / `by 2s` without a `%`; with
both handled the tally is 333 + 2 = **335** rows and Defense Absolute lands on the printed **78** (20 relics) exactly.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 63 (iteration 72: the wave axis becomes one law and the fleet shard columns close)

## Asked

Three uncited pages (`wiki/enemy/enemies` 16 tables, `wiki/enemy/elite-enemies` 2, `wiki/enemy/fleet-enemies` 5, all last
verified 2026-11-01). Two questions: do the Elite-spawn grids have a closed form across tiers (they are the missing input
of the Elite Cell model in T006), and do the fleet reward columns follow a law rather than being hand-set?

## What was established (T006 Rev 12)

**The wave axis is one law.** Both spawn grids print the same grid of *chance rows x tier columns*, and

```
wave(chance, tier T) = base(chance) x 0.9^(T - 1)          (T1 bases: 500 / 1 000 / 1 500 / 2 000 / 3 000 / 4 000 / 5 000 / 6 000 / 7 000 / 8 000)
```

re-adds on **10/10** non-zero rows at T15 (114 / 229 / 343 / 458 / 686 / 915 / 1 144 / 1 373 / 1 601 / 1 830 = the printed
cells) and on **15/15** tiers of the 0.33 % row. The chance ladder itself is a step function: 0.33 % from **W500**, 1.33 %
from **W1 000**, 3 % W1 500, 5 % W2 000, 8 % W3 000, 12 % W4 000, 16 % W5 000, 21 % W6 000, 27 % W7 000, 33 % from
**W8 000**. Practical consequence: the 33 % band starts at **W1 830 in a T14 run** vs **W8 000 at T1** - a 4.4x head start,
which is the arithmetic reason T14+ is the cell-pressing tier rather than a rule of thumb.

**Predicted cells/run** (step-function sum x the printed per-tier averages, so the model is checkable against the on-screen
counter): T1 W1 000 **1.65** elites = 2 cells Â· T5 **5.92** = 18 Â· T10 **20.28** = 112 Â· T11 **23.26** = **140** Â·
T13 **32.92** = 230 Â· T14 **37.63** = **395** Â· T14 W2 000 **223.11** = 2 343 Â· T18 **72.16** = 1 082. At W1 000 the T11
band starts are 174 / 349 / 523 / 697 / 1 046 / â€¦, so depth past W500 is the dominant term, not luck. Printed caps that
limit the yield: **1** elite per wave early (2 later), **8** on screen per type (Scatter children excluded), and immunity to
Orbs / Death Ray / Shockwave / Black Hole.

**The fleet columns close arithmetically** (these are shard income, T010â€™s layer): module shards
`5 + (wave - 1)/250` reproduces all **21** printed band rows and lands on the printed cap **25** at W5 000; reroll shards
**1 080 (T14) to 2 100 (T21)** with deltas **+120 then +150/tier** âœ“; split **80 %** reroll / **20 %** module (5 % per
type). Spawn tables: T1â€“T13 first spawn `15 000 - 250Â·(T-1)` at frequency **100** (T13 = 12 000 âœ“), then T14 2 495 /
T15 1 495 / T16 995 / T17 495 / T18 95 / T19 45 / T20+ **5**, and the bonus-spawn column is `11 750 - 250Â·(T-14)` (all ten
deltas = -250 âœ“). Companion pairs are fixed (Overcharge+Ranged, Commander+Tank, Saboteur+Fast).

**Recorded as a skew, not resolved:** the two spawn-grid pages agree on T1â€“T15 but from **T16** their columns are offset by
one row and differ by Â±1 (`elite-enemies` 102 / 205 / 308 / 411 where `enemies` has 103 / 206 / 412 / 618) - i.e. `floor`
vs `round` of the same `base x 0.9^n`. The doc now says: compute with the 0.9 law, read the panel when a T16+ cell matters.

## Housekeeping

- **T006 â†’ Verified (Rev 12)**: new section â€œThe wave lawsâ€ (law + 8-row prediction table + fleet block + skew note),
  **3** checklist items, **4** falsifiers, **2** source bullets. INDEX row + README row (T006) updated.
- Checks after the edit: 24 README rows / 7 pipes, 0 missing files, 0 bad anchors.

## Verdict

**T006 Rev 12.** **Next:** the build-order guides still un-mined (`devo-guide`, `cf-guide`, `orb-devo`, `smax-devo`,
`hybrid-guide`, `health-build`, `tier-specific-guide`) for the tier-11+ layer, plus `wiki/guide/guides-thinking-about-perks`

# 2026-11-02 - Session 66 (iteration 75: the nine UW+ ledgers close and the stone layer re-adds to 179240)

## Asked

One open hypothesis that turned out to be answerable from sources alone: **H033** (â€œhow many levels does a UW+ ability
have - 10, or 13-15?â€). fandom prints â€œabilities can be upgraded **10** timesâ€ while its own `Template:TableUWPCOST` rows
carry 14 entries, and T008 Â§1's **184 740** depends on which is right. Probe: the nine `wiki/uw/<name>` pages each print an
`UW+ Cost Table`.

## What was established (T008 Rev 5, H033 resolved)

**The caps are per ability, not a constant:** **11** (Smite, Cover Fire) Â· **13** (Chrono Loop) Â· **14** (Golden Combo,
Consume, Kill Wall, Charged Mine, Death Creep) Â· **19** (Light Range). Each `Cost` row's length is the cap, and Chain
Lightning's page prints its own `TOTAL` row (**12 650 + unlock**) which matches the computed sum of its 11 cells exactly.

**The whole layer is 179 240 stones**, so the fandom uniform model (3 x 23 800 + 2 x 18 570 + 4 x 19 050 = 184 740) is high
by **5 500** - exactly the three short caps. The two sources agree wherever the cap is 14: **Consume/Kill Wall 19 050**,
**Charged Mine 18 570**, **Death Creep 23 800** re-add cell for cell. Recomputed pool table: 9 705 (unlocks) + 14 650 (UW+
unlocks) + 179 240 (UW+ levels) + 244 302 (basic stats) = **447 897** for the UW block, **~493 k** with the card/bot/
synchronicity/assist items (was ~500 k), so the 10 700-stones/year horizon is ~46 years rather than 47.

**Every printed `Value` row re-adds from a closed form** (this is what makes the table trustworthy rather than transcribed):

```
Golden Combo   0.03 x (n + 1) %        15/15 cells   (matches the printed formula 1 + 0.0003 x (L+1))
Chrono Loop    0.10 + 0.05n            14/14
Consume        0.05 + 0.05n            15/15
Kill Wall      3 + n(n + 1) / 2        15/15   (triangular)
Light Range    0.01 + 0.01n            ok
Smite          0.05 + 0.05n            12/12
Charged Mine   0.5 + n + 0.2n(n - 1)   15/15   (x50.9 at the cap)
Cover Fire     13 - n                  12/12
Death Creep    120 + 70n               14/15, level 14 prints 1 110 vs the law's 1 100 -> +10 skew
```

**The knee analysis (Â§1.â‘£ point 5).** With cost growing ~x1.2-1.27 per level (GT's printed ratios: 1.200 / 1.194 / 1.186 /
1.216 / 1.210 / 1.200 / 1.222 / 1.227 / 1.222 / 1.242 / 1.268 / 1.269 / 1.258), the seven constant-increment trees are
monotone from level 1 (Golden Combo 10 000 -> 138 333 stones/pp, x13.83; Light Range 40 000 -> 420 000, x10.5; Death Creep
4.3 -> 61.4, x14.3; Chrono Loop x6.13; Consume x6.75; Smite x8.83; Cover Fire x8.83), i.e. â€œbuy the first 1-3 levelsâ€.
**Two trees have an interior minimum** because their own value increments grow: **Kill Wall** bottoms at **level 8**
(1 300 stones for +8 units = **162.5**/unit against 400/unit at L1, still 192.9 at the cap) and **Charged Mine** at
**level 4** (510 for +2.2 = **231.8**/unit). Practical rule now in the doc: push DW's and ILM's UW+ to 8 and 4 before
touching any other ability's deep levels.

**Also new:** the basic-stat tables agree with the old fandom blocks (GT Multiplier 5 / 13 / 22 / 32 / 43 = **115** for
5.0x -> 9.0x, the same 115 already used in Â§6's perk-value check; CL chance +1.5 pp per level at 8 / 26 / 44 / 62 = the
step-18 law again), and level **0** of every UW+ already carries value (GT 0.03 %, BH 0.05x, CL 0.05 %), which is why the
500 â€¦ 3 800 **unlock** is the better first buy than a paid level.

## Housekeeping

- **T008 -> Verified (Rev 5)**: new Â§1.â‘£ (nine-row table + 4 numbered consequences + the knee table), Â§1's pool table
  recomputed (179 240 / 447 897 / ~493 k), **3** checklist items, **2** falsifiers, **1** source bullet.
- **H033 resolved** in its own file with the cap/sum table; **H056** amended (the per-level prices *are* printed, only the
  containment question remains). INDEX rows for T008 + H033 + the session-66 link; README row for T008.

## Verdict

**T008 Rev 5, H033 resolved.** **Next:** the remaining build-order guides (`devo-guide`, `cf-guide`, `orb-devo`,
`smax-devo`, `hybrid-guide`, `health-build`); then the same nine `wiki/uw/*` pages' lab ladders (`Level/Time/Cost/Value`
tables are already on those pages) folded into Â§4's sync ladder. Device-only reads remain **H053**, **H054**, **H055**,
**H056**, T024 Â§6 and the T002/T003/T005 panel confirmations.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 67 (iteration 76: the Golden Combo income matrix closes into two ratio laws)

## Asked

`wiki/uw/golden-tower` (2026-11-01) prints a **41 x 10 relative-income matrix** (durations 40-80 s x GT+ levels 1-10) plus
three prose ratios ("213 %", "504 %", "231 %"). Question: is the matrix a rounded copy of the printed formula, or does it
carry extra structure the formula omits - and does it re-add?

## What was established (T008 Rev 6)

**The prose claims re-add from the cells** (arithmetic, no rounding games): `0.376 / 0.177 = ` **212.4 %** vs the printed
"roughly 213 %"; `2.310 / 0.459 = ` **503.3 %** vs "about 504 %"; `2.310 / 1.000 = ` **231.0 %** vs "around 231 %" (exact;
the third is the anchor cell, so the matrix is normalised to **GT+10 @ 60 s = 1.000**).

**Two ratio laws reproduce the grid.** From each row's nine consecutive level ratios:

```
per GT+ level:  r(D) = exp(0.002399 D - 0.012353)   ->  x1.0872 (40 s) / x1.1406 (60 s) / x1.1967 (80 s)
                all 27 printed consecutive ratios match the fit within 0.0027
per +1 s:       x1.0241 at GT+1  ->  x1.0464 at GT+10   (from the 40 s -> 80 s rows, 40 steps)
```

so duration and UW+ level are **multiplicatively coupled**: one extra second is worth x1.0241 at GT+1 but x1.0464 at GT+10
(a x1.0218 edge per GT+ level), and one GT+ level is worth x1.0872 at 40 s versus x1.1967 at 80 s.

**The decision that follows (now in Â§1.â‘¤).** Because duration scales the value of every other term, the cheap **Duration**
workshop block goes first: its first ten levels price **5 / 14 / 23 / 32 / 41 / 50 / 59 / 68 / 77 / 98 = sum 467 stones**
for **+10 s** (15 s -> 25 s), while a UW+ level starts at 300-400 stones and multiplies less at short durations. Same
"knee-first" logic as Â§3, now quantified on a printed grid instead of inferred from the cost columns.

**Skew, recorded not resolved.** The bare printed formula `((1 + 0.0003 x (L + 1))^Kills) - 1` with `Kills` proportional to
`D` would force `ln r` to be exactly proportional to `D` (intercept 0) and a per-second value independent of `L`; the matrix
has a **-0.0124** intercept and a per-second value that rises with the level, which is the activation-count effect the
page's own 100 %-uptime note describes (activations = run time / duration). The doc now says: use the printed cells, keep the
formula as the shape. Also re-confirmed from this page: **Multiplier 20 / Duration 38 / Cooldown 20** levels totalling
**27 186** stones - an independent restatement of Â§2's GT row.

## Housekeeping

- **T008 -> Verified (Rev 6)**: new Â§1.â‘¤ (anchor-cell table, the two laws, the ordering rule, the skew note), **2** checklist
  items, **1** falsifier, **1** source bullet. INDEX row + session-67 log link; README row for T008.
- Validator after the edit: 24 README rows / 7 pipes, links and anchors clean.

## Verdict

**T008 Rev 6.** **Next:** the other eight `wiki/uw/*` pages' lab ladders (the `Level / Time / Cost / Value` columns - the GT
pair already seen reads **1 M / 1.31 M / 1.851 M / 3.554 M / 8.503 M** with times 40 h -> 75.8 h, so this family does
**not** follow the x1.5 / x1.15 clock law and needs its own fit), folded into Â§4's sync ladder; then the remaining
build-order guides. Device-only reads remain **H053**, **H054**, **H055**, **H056**, T024 Â§6.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 68 (iteration 77: the UW-category lab ladders get a per-level law and a level-10 knee)

## Asked

The queued item from session 66: the `Level / Time / Cost / Value` tables on the `wiki/uw/*` pages. Two questions: are those
columns per level or cumulative (H050 was settled for the *main* category only), and is there a law for the family, since
the numbers match neither x1.5 nor x1.15?

## What was established (T006 Rev 14)

**Per level, decisively.** The DW line's printed costs are exactly geometric - **x2.25** at every one of the nine steps
(2.2500, 2.2500, 2.2519, 2.2456, 2.2539, 2.2478, 2.2506, 2.2501, 2.2502 with the last three cells rounded to 3 significant
digits) and its times exactly **x1.2544**. A cumulative column cannot be geometric, so these tables are per level like the
main category's.

**Sibling lines are offset, not rescaled.** Over the ten levels the three pages share: `DW(n) = GT(n) - 750 000` for **all
10** cells, and `BH(n) = GT(n) + 19 000 000 + 500 000(n - 1)` exactly; the time columns offset by a flat **20 h** per level.
That is why one printed ladder prices most UW labs: read the line's level-1 base, add the shared growth, add the line's own
linear term.

**Line bills (summed from the printed columns).** GT Bonus **25** lv (**+0.15**/lv, +3.75): **124.85 B** coins /
**466.57 d**; GT Duration **20** lv (+1 s/lv, +20 s): **34.39 B** / **214.06 d**; BH's x-line **20** lv (value `0.5n + 1`,
x1.5 -> x11): **91.92 B** / **214.05 d**; DW's x2.25 line **10** lv: **265.93 T** / **283.23 d**; plus the single-level
unlocks at **15 B / 17 d 8 h 40 m** and **550 B / 28 d 22 h 27 m**.

**The knee, which is the practical payoff.** GT Bonus' first **10** levels cost **608.03 M** = **0.5 %** of the 25-level
bill while carrying **40 %** of the value steps (10 of 25), and take **882.7 h** of **11 197.6 h** = **7.9 %** of the time;
levels 11-25 cost **124.24 B** for the same +0.15 steps (per-level price **277.37 M** at L10, **8.84 B** at L20, **26.19 B**
at L25). So "ten and stop" is not a rule of thumb here, it is a 1 : 200 price ratio between the two halves of the line -
the same knee-first logic as Â§3 measured on the UW category.

**Caveat recorded.** GT's and DW's ladders agree only through level **10** (L11: 450.68 M vs 334.62 M), so the shared-ladder
claim is stated for the first ten levels, which is exactly the range the knee rule uses.

## Housekeeping

# 2026-11-02 - Session 69 (iteration 78: nine weapons' lab tables collapse to six sequences and 2.25 is 1.5 squared)

## Asked

The queued item: do the remaining six `wiki/uw/*` pages' `Level / Time / Cost / Value` tables join the GT/BH/DW offset
pattern, or does each weapon price its labs independently? If they share sequences, one printed ladder becomes a lookup sheet
for the whole category.

## What was established (T006 Rev 15)

**Census.** The nine pages print **39** lab tables (GT 2, BH 4, DW 5, Spotlight 2, CF 4, CL 5, ILM 4, SM 7, PS 6). Every
multi-level line sampled (>= 10 levels) belongs to one of **six** sequences, and five of those are exact offsets of another
(max error **0** on the printed ranges):

```
L-I   base 1 000,000  GT Bonus / GT Duration                      124.85 B / 466.57 d (25 lv)
L-II  base   250,000  = L-I - 750 000 ; clock = L-I - 20 h        124.84 B / 445.73 d (25 lv)
L-III base   250,000  own sequence ; clock = L-I - (15 + 5n) h    116.24 B / 825.44 d (30 lv)
L-IV  base 100.00B    cost x1.5, clock x1.12                       38.34q / 2011.11 d (30 lv)
L-V   base 100.00B    cost x2.25, clock x1.2544                   265.93 T / 283.23 d (10 lv)
L-VI  base 1.00q      cost x2.05, clock + 400 h (arithmetic)     1247.44q / 916.67 d (10 lv)
```

**Checks:** `DW(n) = GT(n) - 750 000` 12/12 and `-20 h` 25/25; SM's two variant lines `= L-II + 200 000 + 50 000n` (12/12)
and `= L-III + 550 000` (15/15); Spotlight `= 200 000 000 + (L-I - 1 000 000)` (12/12); the CF page's rotation-rate and
slow-% lines are the **same** cost/clock columns with different Value text (30 levels each).

**The nicest closed form.** `L-V(n) = L-IV(2n - 1)` for **all 20** cells (10 cost + 10 time): the DW line is literally the
odd levels of the Chain Lightning / Poison Swamp ladder, which is why its steps are exactly the **squares** 2.25 = 1.5Â² and
1.2544 = 1.12Â². Two "different" ladders in the UI are one ladder sampled at a different stride.

**Two distinct sequences share the 250 000 starting price** (L-II: 250 000 / 560 000 / 1 101 067 ; L-III: 250 000 / 285 000 /
496 983), so the level-1 price alone does not identify a line - level 2 is the discriminator. Noted as a lookup rule.

**Knee, now on four price shapes.** First half of the line vs the whole bill: L-I **0.5 %** / 40 %, L-II **0.48 %** / 40 %,
L-III **0.25 %** / 33 %, L-IV **0.03 %** / 33 %, L-VI **2.7 %** / 50 %. On L-IV the per-level price runs 3.84T at L10 to
221.68T at L20 for the identical +3 % step. "Hold every UW line at 10" is a measured rule, not a heuristic.

## Housekeeping

- **T006 -> Verified (Rev 15)**: new sub-section "The nine-weapon census" (family table + the five laws + the knee table),

  `tools/patch-readme-78.ps1`.
- New helper `tools/parse-uw-labs.ps1 -File <dump> -Rows 6,8,...` prints the `C: / T: / V:` columns of the chosen payload
  rows (the tables are 3-significant-digit after ~level 12, which is why the laws are stated on the printed range).

## Verdict

**T006 Rev 15.** **Next:** the build-order guides (`devo-guide`, `cf-guide`, `orb-devo`, `smax-devo`, `hybrid-guide`,
`health-build`) into T003's build layer; then the remaining single-level unlock lines and the two `Smart Missile` own-sequence

# 2026-11-02 - Session 73 (iteration 82: the nine Ultimate Weapon cost rows are four sequences and the 179 240 ledger closes at every depth)

## Asked

The nine UW+ ability tables have **no** per-page `TOTAL` row (unlike the workshop tables of session 72), so their Î£
(**179 240**) rested on nine separate hand-reads. Are those `Cost` rows actually independent - or do they share step
sequences that validate each other across trees?

## What was established (T008 Rev 8)

**Four sequences, not nine.** Reading `step(n) = cost(n) âˆ’ cost(nâˆ’1)` off the transposed `Level / Cost / Value` rows:

- **A** `60 70 80 110 130 150 200 250 300 400 550 700 850` â†’ **Î£ 14 = 20 070** (Golden Combo).
- **Aâ€²** identical for the first ten cells (Î£ 10 = **7 970** both), then `350 400 450 500` â†’ **Î£ 14 = 18 570** (Charged Mine).
  So **A âˆ’ Aâ€² = 1 500**, cell by cell **50 / 200 / 450 / 800** - the two 14-level trees cost the *same* 7 970 for levels 1â€“10.
- **B** `100 110 120 130 140 150 150 200 200 250 250 250 250 300 300 300 300 300` â†’ **Î£ 13 = 16 350** (Chrono Loop),
  **Î£ 14 = 19 050** (Consume, Kill Wall), **Î£ 19 = 37 050** (Light Range). One row, three depths.
- **C** `75 100 125 125 200 225 300 350 400 450 500 550 600` â†’ **Î£ 11 = 12 650** (Smite, Cover Fire),
  **Î£ 14 = 23 800** (Death Creep). Two depths.

**Why this is a real check:** because B appears at three caps and C at two, the same sequence has to be right at five
different truncations - a mis-read cell breaks several sums at once. The nine partial sums re-add to
`20 070 + 16 350 + 19 050 + 19 050 + 37 050 + 12 650 + 18 570 + 12 650 + 23 800 =` **179 240**, exactly Â§1's line, and the
one page that does print its own total (`Smite`: `TOTAL 12,650+unlock`) equals family C at cap 11 âœ“.

**Tail law.** Every family ends in a **constant-increment** tail: A runs **+150** (`400 â†’ 550 â†’ 700 â†’ 850`), Aâ€² and C share
the **+50** ladder (`300, 350, 400, 450, 500, 550, 600`), B runs **+300 flat** for its last six levels. So the
"Ã—1.2â€“1.27 per level" intuition is a middle-of-tree effect only - Golden Combo's 13 printed ratios span **1.186 â€¦ 1.269**
and its last three are 1.268 / 1.269 / 1.258, i.e. deep levels are nearly *linear* in price, which is why the knee-first rule
(Â§3) keeps paying as the level counter grows.

**Pricing a tree in two taps.** Family is fixed by the **level-1 cost** (**300** â†’ A/Aâ€²/C, **400** â†’ B) and, among the three
300-start trees, by the **level-11 cell** (**2 050** GT+, **2 000** ILM+, **3 150** PS+); the cap then reads the Î£ straight
off the table. Eight of nine trees are fully priced from two printed numbers.

**Two value-row corrections.** Light Range runs **0.01Ã— â€¦ 0.20Ã—** over its 20 printed cells (the row was noted as ending at
0.18Ã— - the law `0.01 + 0.01n` is right at n = 19), and Death Creep's steps are **+70 thirteen times with one +80** at the
last cell (1 030 â†’ 1 110), so that "+10 skew" is a single final cell rather than a systematic offset.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 76 (iteration 85: the wave clock is 26 plus 9 seconds so one wave costs 35 s and tournaments run faster by a fixed factor)

## Asked

Every coin figure in this repo is *per wave* or *per kill*, but nothing printed the length of a wave â€” so the per-wave laws
could not be turned into an hourly rate. `wiki/system/gameplay` prints the timers; the question is whether they close into a
usable clock law together with the already-priced `Game Speed` line.

## What was established (T002 Rev 4)

**The clock:** a normal wave at Ã—1 speed = **26.00 s length stage + 9.00 s cooldown stage** (nothing spawns during the
cooldown; the next wave starts only when *both* timers end) â‡’ **35 s per wave**, split **74.3 % / 25.7 %**. In **tournaments the
cooldown is halved** â‡’ 30.5 s, i.e. exactly **35 / 30.5 = Ã—1.1475** more waves in the same wall-clock time.

> **waves/h = 3600 Ã— s / 35** (normal) and **3600 Ã— s / 30.5** (tournament), where `s` is the selected speed cap.

| s | Ã—1 | Ã—2 | Ã—3 | Ã—4 | Ã—5 | Ã—6.25 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| normal waves/h | **102.86** | 205.71 | **308.57** | 411.43 | **514.29** | **642.86** |
| tournament waves/h | 118.03 | 236.07 | 354.10 | 472.13 | **590.16** | 737.70 |

**It closes against the priced speed leg.** `Game Speed` (7 levels) prints values **Ã—2.0 â€¦ Ã—5.0** â€” the law `1.5 + 0.5 Ã— level`
fits **7/7** â€” for costs re-adding to **1 714 800** coins and times to **65 277 min = 45.33 d** of one lab slot (the same Î£
T020 verified). So **L1â€“L3 (14 800 coins, â‰ˆ 13 h of slot) move the account from 102.86 to 308.57 waves/h**, and that factor
multiplies the CPW *and* the CPK leg of `coins/h â‰ˆ (CPW + kills Ã— CPK) Ã— waves/h` at once â€” the arithmetic reason T020 buys
that line first. Note the Ã—6.25 row is the lab cap Ã—5 **plus** the `Game Speed` perk stack (+1.25, T011).

**Two rules from the same page that change what to tap:**

1. **`wave` â‰  `round`.** A round is the whole run to death, and a once-per-round effect is **not refreshed by restarting or
   advancing waves** â€” only by the tower dying. So `Second Wind`-class charges should be spent against the *expected* death
   wave rather than hoarded across restarts.
2. **Two free readouts:** tap the **top-left currency display** (totals â‡„ **per-minute rates** for Cash/Coins) and tap the
   **wave counter** to open the **Wave Info Menu**, which prints every enemy type with its stats **and how often it spawns** â€”

   Energy Shield charges appear under the gem count.)

**â‰ˆ 309 waves/h** and at the Ã—5 cap **â‰ˆ 514/h** â€” the single multiplier that scales *both* coin legs. **Next:** T024 takes the

# 2026-11-02 - Session 77 (iteration 86: the ELS Reduction tier staircase is exactly 5 times tier minus 13 and the printed 40 pins it to the Perks screen)

## Asked

`system/tiers` printed `ELS Reduction Lvl 5 â€¦ 40` across Tiers 14â€“21 (banked in session 75). Is that column one exact law, and
does it identify *which* object the level belongs to â€” since the game has a **workshop** pair (699 levels), a **lab** line
(Group 4, **10** levels) and a **perk** line all named around â€œEnemy Level Skipâ€?

## What was established (T024 Rev 2)

**One exact staircase.** Printed required levels 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, 35, 40 for Tiers 14â€¦21 = **`5 Ã— (tier âˆ’ 13)`**,
**8/8 exact** â‡’ `ELS Reduction` moves **+5 per tier**, total distance **T14 â†’ T21 = 40 levels**, and it is the **only**
condition printed at every tier that has conditions at all (the two 50-start lines stall at their 95 cap; the `X's Ultimate`
family arrives tier by tier at 20).

**The printed 40 identifies the object.** The `Enemy Level Skip` **lab** line caps at **10** levels (T014 Â§4.1: Group 4, 2 q â†’
1024 q, +1 %/level) and the **workshop** lines at **699**, so a required level of 40 is neither â€” it is the **Perks**-screen
line's level. That is a structural identification from the caps alone, and it is the first thing the new checklist item
confirms (Tier 14 â†’ 5, Tier 18 â†’ 25).

**Why the panel number and the tier number must be kept apart.** Â§1's pipeline applies the tier's reduction *after*
`(workshop + lab + module + vault + relic) Ã— enhancement`, so the fully built panel (**62.40 %**) is still scaled down inside
the tier. Putting this staircase into T014 Â§4.2's own law `skip_mult = 1 âˆ’ 0.01 Â· value Â· (1 âˆ’ R)`:

| `R` | Tier 14 (value 5) | Tier 21 (value 40) | T21 Ã· T14 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Ã—0.9500 â‡’ **59.28 %** | Ã—0.6000 â‡’ **37.44 %** | **0.632** |
| 20 % (generic BC lab maxed) | Ã—0.9600 â‡’ 59.90 % | Ã—0.6800 â‡’ **42.43 %** | 0.708 |
| 29 % (+ the dedicated line) | Ã—0.9645 â‡’ 60.18 % | Ã—0.7160 â‡’ **44.68 %** | 0.742 |

so **the same investment is worth 0.63â€“0.74 as much skip at Tier 21 as at Tier 14**, and the BC-reduction labs recover only
â‰ˆ one tenth of that gap (0.632 â†’ 0.742). Two practical rules follow: buy the perk **in blocks of five** (nothing between the
blocks is ever needed), and read the *panel* percentage as tier-independent â€” the two new checklist items test exactly that
pair.

## Housekeeping

- **T024 â†’ Verified (Rev 2)**: new **Â§3.â‘ ** (the 8-row staircase + four consequences + the `skip_mult` table), **2** checklist
  items, **1** Â§6 open term (level vs percentage value, with the separating read), **3** falsifiers (staircase â‰  law â‡’ take
  the block size from two panel reads; a percentage beside the condition â‡’ the tables are misaligned; a tier-dependent panel
  percentage â‡’ drop the `skip_mult` column), header Rev line + source `wiki/system/tiers`.
- INDEX row (Rev 1 â†’ **Rev 2**) + session-77 anchor; README T024 row via `tools/patch-readme-86.ps1`.

## Verdict

**T024 Rev 2.** The skip layer now has both halves: the *supply* side (workshop/lab/relic/enhancement â†’ **62.40 %**, verified
in Rev 1) and the *demand* side (the tier staircase `5 Ã— (tier âˆ’ 13)`, verified 8/8) â€” which is what makes the buying order
non-obvious: the first 40 levels of the perk are five-per-tier, not a single push. **Next:** the remaining `wiki/system/*`
prose pages (`in-game-support`, `profile`, `themes-menu`) for stray caps, then T011 can take the per-tier perk bill as its
own ladder. Device-only reads remain **H053**, **H054**, **H055**, **H056**, **H047**, T024 Â§6.

## Asked

The tier tableâ€™s condition column is a list of `Line Lvl N` pairs. Since **one perk pick = one level**, the sum of those
required levels *is* the number of picks a tier costs. Question: does that Î£ behave like a law (so the bill can be predicted
one tier ahead), and what is it worth in waves under the pick-rate tunings of T011 Â§1?

| Tier | 13 | 14 | 15 | 16 | 17 | 18 | 19 | 20 | 21 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Coin bonus | 14.7 | 17.6 | 21.3 | 25.2 | 29.1 | 33 | 40 | 48 | 60 |
| Condition lines | 0 | 6 | 8 | 9 | 10 | 13 | 14 | 15 | 17 |
| **Î£ picks** | **0** | **146** | 242 | 328 | 428 | 585 | 735 | 890 | **1 056** |
| Marginal | â€” | +146 | +96 | +86 | +100 | +157 | +150 | +155 | +166 |

**All eight marginals re-add from the family steps** â€” `marginal = 10 Ã— (the +10 lines still below 95) + 15 Ã— (Ultimate
lines) + 5 (ELS) + Î”More Bosses + the entry levels of that tierâ€™s new lines`:
T15 `4Â·10 + 5 + 1 + 50 = 96` âœ“ Â· T16 `6Â·10 + 5 + 1 + 20 = 86` âœ“ Â· T17 `6Â·10 + 15 + 5 + 20 = 100` âœ“ Â· T18
`6Â·10 + 2Â·15 + 5 + 2 + 60 = 157` âœ“ (the only **+2** step of the hand-set `More Bosses`, and three lines entering at once) Â·
T19 `2Â·5 + 4Â·10 + 5Â·15 + 5 + 20 = 150` âœ“ (here the two 95-bound lines start contributing **5**, not 10) Â· T20
`4Â·10 + 6Â·15 + 5 + 20 = 155` âœ“ Â· T21 `(10+5+10+10) + 7Â·15 + 5 + 21 = 166` âœ“. Every running total also matches the panel sums
printed by the parser, so the ledger is closed **8/8 + 8/8**.

**In waves**, with T011â€™s own pick-rate table: the Tier-14 bill costs **29 200 / 9 125 / 4 424** waves at
**5 / 16 / 33** picks per 1 000 waves (untuned / Active / Near-max) â€” a **6.6Ã—** spread, which is the perk-tuning payoff
expressed as tier progress rather than as coins/min. Tier 18 needs **117 000 / 36 563 / 17 727** waves and Tier 21
**211 200 / 66 000 / 32 000**.

**Two planning rules the arithmetic forces:**

1. Buy the lines in their own step sizes â€” **Ultimates in 15-blocks, the +10 lines in 10-blocks, `ELS Reduction` in 5s** â€”
   a level between the steps is never needed by any tier, so it is the only kind of perk point that can be spent â€œearlyâ€
   without cost, and the cheapest is to stop at the step.
2. **Tiers that introduce several lines at once are the expensive steps**: T18 (+157, three new lines) is worse value per
   pick (**0.0248** bonus/point) than T16 (**0.0453**) or T21 (**0.0723**), even though T18â€™s bonus gain (+3.9) looks like
   T16â€™s â€” the difference is entirely the entry levels (20 each).

# 2026-11-02 - Session 79 (iteration 88: the seven tournament reward ladders are recorded cell-for-cell and ticket buying collapses to n star = floor of P over 10)

## Asked

Â§3 carried only the bottom band and the rank-1 row of each league. Question: what do the **complete ten-band tables** add â€”
is there structure behind the 60â€“70 printed cells (so the pay of an unlisted band can be derived), and can the ticket price
ladder (`10, 20, 30 â€¦`) be turned into one closed-form â€œhow many tickets should I buyâ€ rule?

## What was established (T014 Rev 8)

**The ladders** (Hub `wiki/system/tournaments`, 2026-11-01, six leagues + fandom's seven with Mythic, dated **v29,
2026-08-26**): all **130 cells** of the Hub tables now sit in Â§3.2. Cross-source: **20/20 cells identical for Copper,
Silver and Gold** (the leagues a sub-T11 account plays), 6/20 Platinum, 1/20 Champion, 0/20 Legend â‡’ the low leagues are
doubly confirmed and only the upper ones carry the version skew already logged in Â§3.1. Parsed with the new
**`tools/fandom-rewards-parse.ps1`**, which also confirms **all seven** tables' band widths Î£ to **30**.

**Four structure laws.**
1. Band widths are `1,1,2,2,2,2,2,3,7,8` for Copperâ€“Gold and `1,1,2,2,2,2,2,3,9,6` from Platinum up (Î£ 30 both) â‡’ above Gold
   the tail is re-cut into 9 + 6 players: **fewer pay steps per place**.
2. The rank-1 columns are separate ladders: gems `100,200,300,400,600,800` (Î” +100 Ã—3, then +200 Ã—2) and stones
   `20,40,80,160,320,425` (**Ã—2 four times**, then +105) â‡’ the stone share of a rank-1 ticket climbs **67 % â†’ 84 %** and the
   gems-per-stone ratio collapses 5.00 â†’ 3.75 â†’ 1.88.
3. The ladder flattens on promotion: rank 1 Ã· bottom band = 10Ã— / 5Ã— / 6Ã— then exactly **4Ã—** three times over, so up there
   *league* beats *placement*; concretely **rank 1 in Copper (300 gem-equiv) = bottom-band Platinum (300)** and **rank 1 in
   Gold (1 100) = Champion 16â€“24 (1 100)**.
4. Î£ per bracket: **885 / 1 845 / 3 010 / 5 225 / 7 850 / 12 175** gems and **251 / 525 / 904 / 1 771 / 3 710 / 7 570**
   stones, **137** Legend keys â‡’ per-player means 29.50 â†’ 405.83 gems, and the **mean is above the median band's pay in every
   league** (Copper 29.50 vs 20 â‡’ 1.48Ã—, Legend 405.83 vs 400 â‡’ 1.01Ã—), so a mid-field finish is *below* the bracket average.
   Bonus: the bottom-band **stone floor is flat at 20 from Gold to Champion** â‡’ there, promotion is a gems-only upgrade and
   the stone jump lives in the 16â€“24 band (50 / 90 / 225).

**The ticket law.** With `P = gems + 10 Ã— stones` for your band, `n` bought tickets cost `5n(n + 1)` and `t = 2 + n` tickets
(free + ad + bought) return `PÂ·t`, so `net(n) = P(n + 2) âˆ’ 5n(n + 1)` is a concave parabola with `net(n) âˆ’ net(n âˆ’ 1) =
P mod 10` â‡’ **buy `n* = floor(P / 10)`** and the return is **â‰¥ Ã—2.00** (worked: Copper bottom `P = 60` â‡’ 6 tickets, 210 in /
480 out; Gold bottom 25 â‡’ 3 250 in / 6 750 out; Legend rank 1 5 05 â‡’ 1 277 650 in / 2 560 350 out). The stones side is where
it compounds â€” `t Ã— band stones` per bracket, twice a week: **80/week** at the Copper bottom band vs **6 400/week** at
Platinum 16â€“24, i.e. **11.4 â†’ 0.14 weeks** to fund the 910-stone GT+DW+BH sync.

**Second ceiling, and the coincidence.** A bracket is 72 h and a tournament run costs 30.5 s per wave (T002), so
`runs â‰¤ 72 Ã· (30.5 W/3 600)`: **W = 500 â‡’ â‰ˆ 17**, **W = 1 000 â‡’ 8**, **W = 2 000 â‡’ 4**. For the target reader the gem optimum
(8 tickets) and the clock optimum (8 runs of wave 1 000) **meet** â€” â€œ6 bought tickets + free + ad, â‰ˆ wave 1 000 eachâ€ is both.

# 2026-11-02 - Session 80 (iteration 89: the milestone skin table obeys paid iff tier is 2 mod 3 so the free cosmetic leg collapses to one formula)

## Asked

The cosmetic coin line is banked (T013 Rev 5: `1 + 0.004 T + 0.008 B + 0.006 (M+G+songs)`), but its bucket totals were built
from *subset* tables (43 event + 8 guild = 51 backgrounds). Question: do the three remaining `wiki/system/*` prose pages
(`themes-menu`, `profile`, `in-game-support`) print anything that closes that ledger, and does the per-tier skin table obey a
law rather than a list?

## What was established (T013 Rev 6)

**Two laws, 21/21 each.** The page prints a 24-row `Tier / Tower skin / Free-Paid` table (tiers 22â€“24 print â€œ-â€). Over the
**21** classified rows: `paid â‡” tier â‰¡ 2 (mod 3)` holds **21/21** (paid = 2, 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20) and therefore the number of
**free** skins by Tier *N* is `N âˆ’ âŒŠ(N+1)/3âŒ‹`, also **21/21** against the table â‡’ **Tier 11 = 7 skins = +2.8 pp, Tier 13 =
9 = +3.6 pp, all 21 tiers = 14 = +5.6 pp**, at the printed unlock **wave 2 500** of each tier (T019â€™s ladder already ends a
tier at 2 500 = skin, 4 500 = relic, so the two agree). Each skin is +0.4 pp and applies **unequipped**.

**The counts are printed, so the subset arithmetic is retired.** The same page states **83** tower skins, **58**
backgrounds, **44** songs (of which only the **3** shop songs count, **200 medals** each, non-rotating), and menu/guardian
themes come from the **Guild** shop. Recomputing Â§1â€™s table with the printed 58 backgrounds (was 51): tower **+33.2**,
backgrounds **+46.4**, menu **+4.8**, guardian **+9.0**, songs **+1.8** â‡’ **95.2 pp**, i.e. a **Ã—1.952** permanent coin
multiplier (Rev 5 said +89.6 pp / Ã—1.90 â€” the 7 extra backgrounds are exactly the **+5.6 pp** difference).

**The half-pair ordering is new.** The pair rate (400 medals for 1.2 pp = 333 medals/pp) hides an asymmetry between its
halves: background `250 / 0.8 = 312.5` medals per pp, tower skin `150 / 0.4 = 375`, song `200 / 0.6 = 333.3` â‡’ when only part
of an eventâ€™s cosmetics fit the budget, take **background â†’ song â†’ tower skin**, and the free milestone skins (0 medals)
still beat all three. This is the only ordering inside the cosmetic shop; whole items remain rate-neutral.

**Where to read it:** the total is on the **tier multiplier menu**; the **Profile** screen (tap the **tower** on the home
menu) only *displays* **1 skin + 5 relics** and â€” printed â€” â€œdisplaying a relic or skin here does not activate it or grant
its effectsâ€, so the Profile is not the place to verify the multiplier. Support tickets go through **Settings â†’ Support**

# 2026-11-02 - Session 87 (iteration 96: eighteen cheap UW labs get one bill and two ladder relations)

## Asked

Appendix A (T006 Rev 16) priced every UW lab line at **full depth** but left three things open: whether the `Value` column is
arithmetic on all lines (the appendix checked the **price/time** shapes only), what the **first ten levels** of a line cost as
a package â€” the number a sub-T11 player actually needs â€” and whether the "lines are offset versions of one family" claim
holds on the **clock** as well as the coins.

## Sources

- Tower Hub `wiki/uw/black-hole` Â· `chain-lightning` Â· `chrono-field` Â· `death-wave` Â· `golden-tower` Â·
  `inner-land-mines` Â· `poison-swamp` Â· `smart-missiles` Â· `spotlight` (all "last verified 2026-11-01", pulled today) â€”
  **6** data tables on the Black Hole page, up to **8** on Smart Missiles; parsed with the new
  `tools/uw-lab-census.ps1` (per-table `rows / L1 / last / step / Î£ / Î£ L1â€“10 / coins-per-point`) and spot-checked row by row
  with `tools/card-tables-parse.ps1`.
- Cross-checks against the existing closed layers: T006 Rev 15's six families, T020 Â§1.7's "constant or constant + linear"
  workshop offset pattern, T008 Â§2's workshop stone totals.

## What was established

**A. The `Value` column is arithmetic everywhere (T006 Appendix A.1).** For **29/29** numeric value columns
`cap = v1 + step Ã— (levels âˆ’ 1)` reproduces the printed cap exactly, e.g. `Shock Chance` 3.00 % â†’ 17.50 % at step 0.50 over
30 levels, `Swamp Stun Time` 1.3 â†’ 10.0 at step 0.3, `Death Wave Damage Amplifier` 6.5 â†’ 50.00 at step 1.5. Two columns are
non-arithmetic by design and both are *descending*: `Spotlight Missiles` **19 â†’ 2** (i.e. `20 âˆ’ n`) and
`Recharge Missile Barrage` **1 500 â†’ 200** (âˆ’250 four times, then âˆ’150 twice â€” piecewise, hand-set). `Swamp Rend -
Additional Enemies` is text-valued (enemy types).

**B. The beginner's bill.** The **18** lines whose level-1 price is â‰¤ 1 M coins â€” the four 250 Kâ€“1 M families â€” sum over their
**first ten levels each** to **8.10 B coins** and **462.33 d** of base slot time â‡’ at five slots with the Ã—3.725 speed stack
â‰ˆ **24.8 d of real time**. The package per line is family-fixed: **600.53 M / 28.45 d** for each of the seven L-II labs,
**287.47 M / 19.07 d** for each of the six L-III labs, plus six offset/on-hand variants (2.173 B / 148.8 d). Everything else in
the layer sits on 100 B / 1 B / 200 M / q bases, i.e. **â‰¥ 20Ã—** one cheap line, so the ten-level pass over the cheap tier *is*
the early UW-lab plan.

**C. Relation â‘  â€” the two 250 K ladders differ by exactly 5 h per level.** Printed per-level times for L-II are
`8.35 Â· 8.53 Â· 9.03 Â· 9.90 Â· 11.28 h` and for L-III `3.35 Â· 3.53 Â· 4.03 Â· 4.90 Â· 6.28 h`; the difference is
`5, 5, 5, 5, 5` h. Over L1â€“L10 that forces a gap of `5 h Ã— 45 = 225 h = 9.375 d`, and the printed sums differ by
`28.45 âˆ’ 19.07 = 9.38 d` âœ“. This check is **reading-independent** (it holds for the per-level and the cumulative reading of
the `Time` column alike), so it is the cleanest confirmation of H050's two candidate conventions.

**D. Relation â‘¡ â€” a line is a family plus an offset, on coins.** `Missiles Radius` (base 800,000) âˆ’ L-III (base 250,000) =
**550,000** on all six compared levels; `Missile Amplifier` (base 500,000) âˆ’ L-II = **250 k, 300 k, 350 k, 400 k, 450 k,
500 k** = `200 000 + 50 000Â·n`. Same shape as the workshop lines (T020 Â§1.7), which means one printed ladder can forecast the
next line's price list to the coin once its base is read.

**E. Corrections to Appendix A** (same pull, so they are print-version moves, not contradictions): the three `Swamp` lines
(`Radius`, `Stun Chance`, `Stun Time`) are **30**-level at **116.24 B / 825.4 d** â€” Appendix A's "20 lv / 12.94 B" is their
20-level **prefix** (verified: the L-III 20-level prefix *is* 12.94 B, which is what the 20-level `Missile Despawn Time`
sums to); `Swamp Rend - Additional Enemies` prints **11** rows with its 11th at **1.00 Q / 83 d 8 h** â‡’ bill **1.01 Q**, not
the 7.49 T recorded at Rev 16; and `Death Wave Health` sums to **15.79 B** over its 30 printed levels (4.40 B = the L1â€“19
prefix).

**F. Coins per printed point** (Î£ L1â€“10 Ã· the value gained) gives the tie-break when two labs compete for one slot. Within
percentage-point lines: `Death Wave Health` **2.40 M/pp** â‰ª `Swamp Stun Chance` **11.50 M/pp** < `Swamp Radius` 23.96 <
`Chrono Field Duration` and `Missile Despawn Time` 28.75 < `Chrono Field Reduction %` 57.49 < `Shock Chance` 120.11 <
`Chain Thunder` 125.87. Within multiplier lines: `Spotlight Coin Bonus` **147.13 M/unit** beats `Black Hole Coin Bonus`
294.25 M, `Missile Amplifier` 403.52 M beats `Shock Multiplier` 1 501.33 M.

## Next open question

The per-point ranking is only valid inside one unit class (pp vs multiplier vs seconds). The unresolved device reads stay

## Verdict

**T006 Rev 18.** The UW lab layer now has a *value* law (29/29), a single number for the beginner's first pass
(**8.10 B coins / â‰ˆ 24.8 d of real time for the ten-level tier of the 18 cheap labs**), two exact ladder relations usable as
integrity checks on any new print (5 h/level; family + offset), and three corrected caps. **Next:** the remaining
`wiki/workshop/*` per-stat pages (they own Â§3's deep-line knees) plus `workshop-respec` / `workshop-upgrades`, then the
